# Alternative Text Descriptions for Health Indicator Charts (Cases 1–50)

This document provides 50 long alternative text descriptions, one for each chart in the dataset. Each description is designed for non-visual access and follows a standardised structure to ensure interpretive clarity without requiring sight of the chart.

## Chart 1: Indicadors de salut per categoria (00022)

### Overview and main message

This chart compares health indicators across 12 healthcare categories, broken down by three status groups: Resolts, Amb seguiment, Derivats. The category with the highest average is Atenció primària (205.3 milers de pacients), while Cardiologia has the lowest (68.8 milers de pacients).

### Chart structure

This is a Clustered Column chart. The X-axis represents 12 healthcare categories: Atenció primària, Urgències, Hospitalització, Diagnòstic, Rehabilitació, Salut mental, Farmàcia, Oncologia, Pediatria, Geriatria, Cirurgia, Cardiologia. The Y-axis represents values in milers de pacients, ranging from 54.9 to 230.8. Each category is subdivided into 3 data series (Resolts, Amb seguiment, Derivats), represented as grouped columns.

### Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons

Across all categories, the Derivats series consistently records the highest values (average: 158.4 milers de pacients), followed by Amb seguiment (134.3 milers de pacients) and Resolts (113.4 milers de pacients). There is a substantial spread of 136.5 milers de pacients between the highest-scoring category (Atenció primària) and the lowest (Cardiologia), indicating meaningful differences across healthcare areas. Overall variability is moderate with a coefficient of variation of 0.35 across all 36 data points.

### Essential key details

The overall minimum value is 54.9 milers de pacients and the maximum is 230.8 milers de pacients. The global average across all 36 data points is 135.4 milers de pacients, with a median of 133.8 and a standard deviation of 47.8. By series, averages are: Derivats at 158.4, Amb seguiment at 134.3, and Resolts at 113.4 milers de pacients.

## Chart 8: Evolució de salut per període (00090)

### Overview and main message

This chart tracks the evolution of a health indicator measured in milers de pacients across 14 time periods, from 2012 to 2025. There is no clear linear trend over time (correlation: 0.10). The data peaks at 104.9 in 2019 and returns to values near the starting level, suggesting a rise-and-fall pattern.

### Chart structure

This is a Clustered Column chart. The X-axis represents time periods, spanning from 2012 to 2025 (14 periods). The Y-axis represents values in milers de pacients, ranging from 56 to 104.9.

### Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons

The data does not follow a linear trend (correlation near zero at 0.10), suggesting fluctuation or a peak-and-decline pattern rather than steady growth or decline. The peak value of 104.9 milers de pacients occurs in 2019, while the lowest value of 56 milers de pacients is recorded in 2025. Variability across periods is moderate (coefficient of variation: 0.25).

### Essential key details

The overall minimum value is 56 milers de pacients and the maximum is 104.9 milers de pacients. The global average across all 14 data points is 69.3 milers de pacients, with a median of 60.3 and a standard deviation of 17.5. The linear correlation between time and the indicator value is 0.10.

## Chart 40: Evolució de web analytics per període (02898)

### Overview and main message

This Area chart tracks the evolution of a web analytics indicator measured in milers de sessions across 15 time periods, from 2011 to 2025, broken down by three engagement levels (Rebot baix, Navegació mitjana, Conversió). The data shows a strong downward trend (correlation: -0.90).

### Chart structure

This is a Area chart. The X-axis represents time periods, spanning from 2011 to 2025 (15 periods). The Y-axis represents values in milers de sessions, ranging from 66.3 to 254.5. Each period includes 3 data series (Rebot baix, Navegació mitjana, Conversió), representing engagement levels. As an area chart, the filled regions beneath each line emphasise the magnitude of values over the sequence.

### Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons

Across all time periods, the Conversió series consistently records the highest values (average: 180.8 milers de sessions), followed by Navegació mitjana (155.2 milers de sessions) and Rebot baix (130.2 milers de sessions). The dominant pattern is a consistent downward trajectory (correlation: -0.90), peaking at 230.3 milers de sessions in 2011 and reaching a trough at 89.6 milers de sessions in 2025. Overall variability is moderate with a coefficient of variation of 0.32 across all 45 data points.

### Essential key details

The overall minimum value is 66.3 milers de sessions and the maximum is 254.5 milers de sessions. The global average across all 45 data points is 155.4 milers de sessions, with a median of 157.9 and a standard deviation of 49.4. By engagement levels, averages are: Conversió at 180.8, Navegació mitjana at 155.2, and Rebot baix at 130.2 milers de sessions. The linear correlation between time and the indicator value is -0.90.

## Chart 401 — CASE_04003
### Title: Compared Profile of Health Indicators (04003)

**Overview and main message**

This chart compares the performance profiles of three health service outcome categories — resolved cases, cases under follow-up, and referred cases — across six health quality dimensions. The main message is a pattern with one dominant dimension per series: resolved and referred cases both peak strongly on accessibility, while follow-up cases peak on resolution rate — creating a consistent opposition on the accessibility–resolution axis between the two groups.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a radar chart with markers at each data point. Three data series are represented: "Resolts" (resolved), "Amb seguiment" (under follow-up), and "Derivats" (referred). Each series is scored across six dimensions: accessibility (Accessibilitat), quality (Qualitat), waiting time (Temps d'espera), resolution (Resolució), satisfaction (Satisfacció), and prevention (Prevenció). The markers make each individual score precisely identifiable alongside the overall profile shape.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Resolved cases score highest on accessibility (76.5) and quality (74.7) but lowest on resolution (39.8). Cases under follow-up score highest on resolution (81.6) and satisfaction (74.4) but lowest on accessibility (45.6). Referred cases also peak on accessibility (79.1) and quality (74.0) but trough on resolution (43.3). Resolved and referred cases share near-identical profile shapes, both strong on accessibility and quality but weak on resolution — while follow-up cases invert this pattern.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 81.6 for resolution in cases under follow-up. The overall minimum is 39.8 for resolution in resolved cases. Average scores across the six dimensions are approximately 60.9 (resolved), 62.9 (under follow-up), and 62.4 (referred). All three averages are very similar, confirming that the difference lies in dimensional distribution, not overall performance level.

## Chart 402 — CASE_04013
### Title: Compared Profile of Manufacturing Indicators (04013)

**Overview and main message**

This chart compares the performance profiles of three manufacturing outcome categories — good units, reprocessed units, and discarded units — across six quality dimensions. The main message is a spiky, strongly contrasted profile: each category scores very high on two or three dimensions and very low on others, with good and discarded units sharing a similar shape while reprocessed units show an opposing profile.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a radar chart with markers. Three data series are represented: "Bona" (good), "Reprocessada" (reprocessed), and "Descartada" (discarded). Each series is scored across six dimensions: quality (Qualitat), productivity (Productivitat), safety (Seguretat), maintenance (Manteniment), cost (Cost), and flexibility (Flexibilitat). Markers identify each individual score with precision.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Good units score highest on quality (90.7) and productivity (80.1) but lowest on maintenance (38.9). Reprocessed units score highest on maintenance (87.6) and cost (79.5) but lowest on quality (30.6). Discarded units score highest on quality (81.1) and flexibility (69.5) but lowest on maintenance (34.6). Good and discarded units are similar in profile shape — both strong on quality and weak on maintenance — while reprocessed units are near-inversely related to both on the quality–maintenance axis.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 90.7 for quality in good units. The overall minimum is 30.6 for quality in reprocessed units. Average scores across the six dimensions are approximately 64.8 (good), 61.4 (reprocessed), and 60.3 (discarded). Good units have the highest average profile, though the distributions are highly contrasted across dimensions.

## Chart 403 — CASE_04022
### Title: Compared Profile of Operations Indicators (04022)

**Overview and main message**

This chart compares the performance profiles of three operational categories — planned, executed, and incidents — across five dimensions. The main message is two strongly contrasted series: planned operations peak on service and bottom out on cost, while executed operations invert this exactly, peaking on cost and bottoming on quality. Incidents form a distinct third profile, peaking on quality.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a radar chart with markers. Three data series are represented: "Planificat" (planned), "Executat" (executed), and "Incidències" (incidents). Each series is scored across five dimensions: service (Servei), time (Temps), cost (Cost), capacity (Capacitat), and quality (Qualitat). Markers identify each individual score.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Planned operations score highest on service (86.8) and lowest on cost (24.4). Executed operations score highest on cost (87.4) and lowest on quality (28.3). The service–cost contrast between planned and executed is very sharp. Incidents score highest on quality (83.4) but lowest on time (27.9), providing a third distinct profile. Each of the three categories has a unique dominant dimension.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 87.4 for cost in executed operations. The overall minimum is 24.4 for cost in planned operations. Average scores are approximately 49.0 (planned), 53.4 (executed), and 49.2 (incidents). Executed operations record the highest average profile, driven primarily by the strong cost dimension.

## Chart 404 — CASE_04028

The main message is a balanced profile pattern: all three institutions score within a moderate and closely clustered range across all eight dimensions, with no extreme peaks or troughs and only modest differences between institution types.

The chart is a radar chart with markers. Three data series are represented: "Públic" (public), "Concertat" (grant-maintained), and "Privat" (private). Each series is scored across eight dimensions: mathematics, reading (Lectura), sciences (Ciències), digital, languages (Idiomes), participation (Participació), equity (Equitat), and academic continuity (Continuïtat acadèmica). Markers allow individual scores to be precisely identified.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Public institutions score highest on reading (68.0) and digital (67.9) but lowest on equity (53.4). Grant-maintained institutions score highest on mathematics (69.5) but lowest on languages (55.6). Private institutions score highest on equity (70.1) — their distinctive strength — and lowest on digital (57.1). All three profiles are broadly similar in shape, with the main differentiation in equity, digital, and languages rather than core academic subjects.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 70.1 for equity in private institutions. The overall minimum is 53.4 for equity in public institutions. Average scores across eight dimensions are approximately 62.3 (public), 62.9 (grant-maintained), and 63.4 (private) — all clustered within 1.1 points of each other, confirming the highly balanced nature of this distribution.

Each series is scored across eight dimensions: service, time, cost, capacity, quality, flexibility, punctuality (Puntualitat), and scalability (Escalabilitat). Markers allow each of the twenty-four individual scores to be precisely identified.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Planned operations peak on service (85.1) and bottom on quality (30.1). Executed operations peak on capacity (88.3) and bottom on scalability (27.3). Incidents peak on punctuality (87.4) and bottom on cost (29.4). No dimension is shared as the highest or lowest across more than one category. The service–capacity contrast between planned and executed (one peaks where the other bottoms) is the most prominent structural feature.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 88.3 for capacity in executed operations. The overall minimum is 27.3 for scalability in executed operations. Average scores are approximately 52.7 (planned), 49.0 (executed), and 47.9 (incidents). Planned operations have the highest average; incidents the lowest. Despite the spread of extremes, all three average profiles are similar in magnitude.

## Chart 451 — CASE_04502
### Title: Operations Indicators by Category (04502)

**Overview and main message**

This chart shows order volumes across three logistics process stages for three operational performance categories. The main message is a late-drop pattern: within each category (planned, executed, incidents), values are lowest at reception and rise progressively to their peak at expedition — but in the broader operational context, the main volume decline would be expected at the final stages of a longer process chain.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a funnel chart with three data series: "Planificat" (planned), "Executat" (executed), and "Incidències" (incidents). Each series spans three process stages: reception, preparation, and expedition. The horizontal axis represents the process stages; the vertical axis measures order volume in thousands of orders (milers de comandes), ranging from 83.3 to 134.7 thousands.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Within each series, values increase progressively from reception to expedition. Planned orders rise from 83.3 (reception) to 96.1 thousand (expedition). Executed orders rise from 102.0 to 113.2 thousand. Incident orders rise from 120.7 to 134.7 thousand. The three series maintain a consistent hierarchy — incidents always exceeding executed, which always exceeds planned — across all three stages.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 134.7 thousand incidents at expedition. The overall minimum is 83.3 thousand planned orders at reception. The gap between incidents and planned at each stage is approximately 37–38 thousand orders, representing a consistent margin between the three operational performance categories.

## Chart 452 — CASE_04503
### Title: Sales Indicators by Category (04503)

**Overview and main message**

This chart shows sales revenue across twelve distribution channels for three regional markets. The main message is a strong-initial-drop pattern: revenue peaks at the distributor and export channels, then drops sharply at direct-to-consumer and flash sale, creating two distinct peaks and two pronounced troughs within each region's channel profile.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a funnel chart with three data series: "Nord" (North), "Centre" (Centre), and "Sud" (South). Each series spans twelve sales channels: physical store, e-commerce, distributor, marketplace, telephone channel, direct-to-consumer, franchise, subscription, export, tender, wholesale, and flash sale. The horizontal axis represents the sales channels; the vertical axis measures revenue in millions of euros (M€), ranging from 21.2 to 41.4 M€.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Within each region, two channels record the highest revenues: distributor (Nord 29.6, Centre 35.5, Sud 41.1 M€) and export (Nord 30.0, Centre 35.8, Sud 41.4 M€). The two lowest channels are direct-to-consumer (Nord 21.4, Centre 27.3, Sud 33.4 M€) and flash sale (Nord 21.2, Centre 27.1, Sud 33.3 M€). The asymmetric wave pattern — peaks at distributor/export, troughs at direct-to-consumer/flash sale — is consistent across all three regions.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 41.4 M€ for export revenue in the South. The overall minimum is 21.2 M€ for flash sale revenue in the North. The South consistently exceeds the Centre by approximately 6 M€ per channel, and the Centre exceeds the North by approximately 6 M€ — confirming a stable regional hierarchy.

## Chart 453 — CASE_04505
### Title: Health Indicators by Category (04505)

**Overview and main message**

This chart shows patient volumes across four healthcare pathway stages for three outcome types. The main message is a clear bottleneck pattern: patient volumes peak at hospitalisation — the most resource-intensive stage — with primary care showing the lowest entry volume and diagnosis showing a secondary drop after hospitalisation.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a funnel chart with three data series: "Resolts" (resolved), "Amb seguiment" (under follow-up), and "Derivats" (referred). Each series spans four stages: primary care, emergencies, hospitalisation, and diagnosis. The vertical axis measures patient volumes in thousands, ranging from 138.1 to 218.4 thousands.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Within each outcome type, the pattern follows: primary care (lowest) → emergencies (high) → hospitalisation (peak/bottleneck) → diagnosis (medium-low). For resolved cases: 138.1 → 152.5 → 153.6 → 140.5 thousand. The bottleneck at hospitalisation is consistent across all three outcome types. Referred cases record the highest volumes throughout (196.9–218.4 thousand), always exceeding follow-up cases, which always exceed resolved cases.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 218.4 thousand for referred cases at hospitalisation — the bottleneck stage. The overall minimum is 138.1 thousand for resolved cases at primary care. Average volumes across four stages are approximately 146.2 thousand (resolved), 175.9 thousand (follow-up), and 208.1 thousand (referred).

## Chart 454 — CASE_04510

The main message is a late-drop pattern: within each series, values peak at expedition and planning, while the lowest values occur at the terminal stages — returns and procurement — confirming that the most significant volume reduction happens late in the process chain.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a funnel chart with three data series: "Planificat" (planned), "Executat" (executed), and "Incidències" (incidents). All three series share the same asymmetric profile: values rise from reception to expedition (first peak), drop at the quality–procurement cluster (lowest at procurement and return), rise again at billing–planning (second peak), and drop at warehouse and return (final minimum). For planned orders: expedition and planning peak at ~28.8 and ~29.1 thousand; return and procurement trough at ~22.2 and ~22.3 thousand. The late-drop pattern — where the lowest values occur at the final stage (return) — is consistent across all three series.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 42.0 thousand for incidents at planning. The overall minimum is 22.2 thousand for planned orders at return. The spread within each series (from minimum to maximum) is approximately 7 thousand orders (planned), 9 thousand (executed), and 10 thousand (incidents).

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

All three series rise from reception to expedition (the peak), then decline at quality and reach their minimum at returns. Planned orders: 64.4 → 74.8 → 75.7 → 66.1 → 54.9 thousand. Incidents: 93.6 → 102.9 → 103.7 → 95.2 → 85.3 thousand. The returns stage records the lowest volume in every series, confirming the late-drop pattern. The drop from expedition to returns is approximately 19–21% across all three series.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 103.7 thousand for incidents at expedition. The overall minimum is 54.9 thousand for planned orders at returns. The reduction from expedition (peak) to returns (trough) within the planned series is 20.8 thousand orders — a 27% decline concentrated in the final two stages.

## Chart 471 — CASE_04676
### Title: Web Analytics Indicators by Category (04676)

**Overview and main message**

This chart shows web session volumes across nine traffic source categories for three session quality tiers. The main message is a uniform-drop pattern: push notification and social channels record the highest session volumes, paid search records the lowest, and the proportional reduction from conversion to low bounce is consistent across all nine sources.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a funnel chart with three data series: "Rebot baix" (low bounce), "Navegació mitjana" (average navigation), and "Conversió" (conversion). Each series spans nine traffic sources: direct, organic, social, email, referral, paid search, affiliate, display, and push. The vertical axis measures session volumes in thousands, ranging from 224.0 to 412.4 thousands.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Push notifications record the highest volumes across all three tiers (302.0 → 364.9 → 412.4 thousand), followed closely by social (298.8 → 361.3 → 409.6 thousand). Paid search records the lowest volumes (224.0 → 276.5 → 343.8 thousand). The proportional reduction from conversion to low bounce is approximately 27–35% across all nine sources, with paid search showing a slightly steeper reduction.

**Essential key details**

The overall maximum is 412.4 thousand for conversion sessions via push notifications. The overall minimum is 224.0 thousand for low bounce sessions via paid search. Average volumes are approximately 269.0 thousand (low bounce), 329.1 thousand (average navigation), and 382.7 thousand (conversion).

## Chart 476 — CASE_04761
### Title: Energy Evolution by Period (04761)

**Overview and main message**

This chart tracks monthly energy generation over twelve years from 2014 to 2025, comparing actual generation against a fixed target benchmark. The main message is a consistent upward growth trajectory that starts well below the target, crosses it in 2019, and continues rising above it through 2025 — demonstrating a sustained expansion of energy generation capacity over the period.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a Clustered Column + Line chart with two series. The primary series "Primària" is displayed as columns representing actual monthly energy generation (GWh) for each year. The secondary series "Secundària" is displayed as a flat horizontal line representing a fixed annual target of 1,002.7 GWh. The horizontal axis covers the years 2014 to 2025. The vertical axis measures monthly energy generation in GWh, ranging from 501.9 to 1,619.8 GWh.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

The primary series grows steadily from 501.9 GWh (2014) to 1,619.8 GWh (2025), more than tripling over twelve years. The crossover point where actual generation first meets the target occurs in 2019 (1,002.5 GWh ≈ target 1,002.7 GWh). From 2020 onwards, actual generation consistently exceeds the target, with the margin growing each year. The growth is continuous with no year-on-year decline.

**Essential key details**

The minimum generation is 501.9 GWh in 2014 (approximately half the target). The maximum is 1,619.8 GWh in 2025 (61.5% above the target). The flat target line at 1,002.7 GWh divides the time series into a below-target phase (2014–2018) and an above-target phase (2019–2025).

## Chart 478 — CASE_04790
### Title: Finance Evolution by Period (04790)

**Overview and main message**

This chart tracks monthly billing across four consecutive months from January to April, with a secondary series showing an associated rate metric. The main message is strong and consistent monthly growth: billing rises sharply from 85.0 M€ in January to 211.8 M€ in April — more than doubling — while the secondary rate metric remains essentially flat, indicating that the growth rate itself stays constant while the volume accelerates.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a Clustered Column + Line chart with two series displayed on a shared axis. The primary series "Primària" shows monthly billing (M€) as columns for January through April. The secondary series "Secundària" is displayed as a nearly flat line representing a growth or margin rate, remaining at approximately 26.1–26.5% throughout the four months. The vertical axis spans from 26.1 to 211.8 M€.

The primary billing series increases by approximately 41–42 M€ per month: January (85.0) → February (124.4) → March (166.8) → April (211.8). Each monthly increment is virtually identical (39–45 M€), confirming a linear growth pattern. The secondary rate series (26.1–26.5%) shows no directional trend — remaining essentially constant throughout the four months while the primary volumes nearly triple.

**Essential key details**

The primary series minimum is 85.0 M€ (January) and maximum is 211.8 M€ (April). The secondary rate series ranges narrowly from 26.1% to 26.5%, averaging approximately 26.3%. The combination of linearly growing volume and stable rate is the defining feature of the "growth with rate" pattern.

## Chart 492 — CASE_04937

**Overview and main message**

This chart tracks monthly sales billing over seventeen years from 2009 to 2025, comparing actual billing against a fixed target of 31.9 M€. The main message is sustained long-term growth: billing starts well below the target in 2009, crosses it between 2015 and 2016, and continues rising well above it through 2025 — demonstrating a clear progression from below-target to substantially above-target performance.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a Custom Combo chart with two series. The primary series "Primària" shows actual monthly billing (M€) as columns across seventeen years. The secondary series "Secundària" is a flat horizontal line representing a fixed target of 31.9 M€. The vertical axis ranges from 12.7 to 55.4 M€.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Billing grows from 12.7 M€ (2009) to 55.4 M€ (2025), more than quadrupling over seventeen years. The growth is broadly continuous with a brief plateau in 2019–2020 (+0.2 M€). The crossover above the 31.9 M€ target occurs between 2015 (27.8 M€, below target) and 2016 (33.0 M€, above target). From 2016 onwards, billing consistently exceeds the target by a widening margin.

**Essential key details**

The minimum is 12.7 M€ (2009, 60.2% below target). The maximum is 55.4 M€ (2025, 73.7% above target). The target at 31.9 M€ divides the seventeen-year period: seven years below (2009–2015) and ten years above (2016–2025).

**Overview and main message**

This chart tracks monthly energy generation across five months from January to May, alongside a secondary percentage metric on a very different scale. The main message is rapid and consistent generation growth alongside a highly variable secondary metric — the two series operate on entirely different scales and show contrasting structural behaviours.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a Custom Combo chart with two series on different scales. The primary series shows monthly energy generation (GWh) growing from 1,415.6 to 3,611.5 over five months. The secondary series shows a percentage metric fluctuating between 3.9% and 17.3%. The axis spans from 3.9 to 3,611.5 (combining both scales).

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Primary generation rises consistently: January (1,415.6) → February (2,028.4) → March (2,608.1) → April (3,146.9) → May (3,611.5 GWh). Each monthly increment is approximately 550–600 GWh, confirming near-linear growth. The secondary metric follows the same irregular pattern seen in the "different scales" charts: 10.0% → 16.7% → 17.3% → 11.1% → 3.9% — peaking in March and falling sharply to a minimum in May.

**Essential key details**

The primary minimum is 1,415.6 GWh (January) and maximum is 3,611.5 GWh (May). The secondary minimum is 3.9% (May) and maximum is 17.3% (March). The two series peak at different months (primary in May, secondary in March), confirming they measure different phenomena on very different scales.

## Chart 500 — CASE_04999
### Title: Operations Evolution by Period (04999)

**Overview and main message**

This chart tracks monthly order volumes over ten years from 2016 to 2025, alongside a secondary rate metric remaining nearly flat at approximately 26%. The main message is a non-monotonic order volume profile: volumes rise from 2016 to 2018, then decline through 2021, then recover to a new high in 2024 before slightly declining in 2025 — while the secondary rate stays essentially constant throughout all fluctuations.

**Chart structure**

The chart is a Custom Combo chart with two series. The primary series shows monthly order volumes (thousands) as columns from 2016 to 2025. The secondary series is a nearly flat line at approximately 25.9–26.5%. The primary axis ranges from approximately 54.6 to 69.7 thousand orders.

**Relevant patterns, trends, and comparisons**

Order volumes follow a rise-fall-recovery pattern: 62.0 → 68.5 → 69.1 (peak in 2018) → 63.1 → 56.1 → 54.6 (trough in 2021) → 59.8 → 67.1 → 69.7 (new peak in 2024) → 65.2 (2025). The decline from 2018 to 2021 (−14.5 thousand, −21.0%) is the most significant change. The secondary rate (25.9–26.5%) remains flat throughout all phases of the cycle.

**Essential key details**

The primary minimum is 54.6 thousand (2021). The maximum is 69.7 thousand (2024). The secondary rate averages approximately 26.2% across the ten years. The cyclic behaviour — two peaks (2018, 2024) separated by a trough (2021) — combined with a constant rate defines this "volume and percentage" chart and distinguishes it from the monotonically growing "growth with rate" charts.
